Title: Apologia Ecclesiae Anglicanae; huic novae editioni accedit Epistola celeberrima ad virum nobilem D. Scipionem, patricium Venetum, de Concilio Tridentino conscripta
Creator: John Jewel, Bishop of Salisbury (Latin original of 1562; the 1838 Cambridge edition names no editor on its title page)
Publisher: Cantabrigiae: impensis T. Stevenson; J. H. Parker, Oxonii; J. G. et F. Rivington et J. Hatchard et fil., Londini
Publication Date: 1838 (title page: MDCCCXXXVIII)
Rights: Public Domain (published 1838, more than 95 years ago; archive.org possible-copyright-status NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT)
Language: lat
Source: Internet Archive item apologiaecclesi00jewegoog, file apologiaecclesi00jewegoog_djvu.txt (Google scan of the Oxford University copy, Bodleian), fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, second pass, English and Scottish worlds agent), 2026-10-23; host rights statement: possible-copyright-status NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT; the Google front matter (its usage guidelines) is kept at the head of the file as received
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged (no long-s glyphs were present; doubled spaces left as scanned). Nothing retyped, dropped or corrected. Rights basis restated: this printing's own date, 1838, is more than 95 years before 2026, so the text is public domain in the United States by date; the work itself is of 1562.
Content note: Latin only. Google's front matter, then the title page (line 146 carries the year), then Jewel's Apologia Ecclesiae Anglicanae complete (from about file line 237, in the six parts of the 1562 text, with the edition's running head ECCLESIAE ANGLICANAE and page numbers), followed by the Epistola ad Scipionem de Concilio Tridentino (from about line 5562). SECOND WITNESS for exact wording: the Google OCR was run with an Italian character set, so Latin vowels carry stray accents throughout (excogitari read as 'excogìtarì', gloriantur as 'glorìantur') and the ae ligature is often read as 'sB' or 'iB' (quaecunque as 'qusBCunque'). The text is readable and searchable, and every sample at 10/30/50/70/90 per cent was Latin prose with these systematic faults only, but a Latin quotation must be verified against the page image. The 1812 Pontefract edition (archive.org apologiaecclesi01jewegoog) is much worse and was not vendored. Lady Bacon's 1564 English is vendored separately as jewel_apology-of-the-church-of-england_bacon-morley1888.txt.
